She now knows that a cancelled unpaid booking is deleted for good, the member gets one cancellation email, and putting it back is not possible.

Who drives: Both. She plays the member on the public booking page and reads the inboxes. You play the office and do every Finance click on your second screen. Finance means anyone with the Finance tick, the auditor role or a super admin. In the Western Cape that is the super admins only, and today that is you. She needs no staff rights for this.

Before you start:

  • The workshop is published, dated at least 3 days away (so the "Remind" button still shows), with no bookings yet:
    • Title: TRAINING - Positive Discipline Follow-up - delete after
    • Date: 21 Nov 2026, 09:00 to 13:00
    • Short code: DISC21
  • The short code is the code of up to 6 letters and numbers the system built from the title. It goes into every EFT payment reference and receipt number.
  • Its Application link (the public booking link) is open on her screen.
  • On the second screen, her own inbox for the right address is open. The wrong address is not a real mailbox, so nobody ever receives what goes there.
    • Wrong address (the typo): wrong.address@example.com
    • Right address: <Trainee personal email>
  • From your pre-flight list, have these two test values ready:
    • NAP number: test member NAP number 05
    • Cell: test cell number 9
  • Optional: talk about the member portal for <Trainee personal email>. This is words only. No portal account is made for training.
  • The hand-off sentence card lies face down.
  • As Coach, read her brief aloud: "You are Busisiwe Nkosi, a NAPTOSA member. You booked a TRAINING workshop by EFT but typed your email wrong, so nothing arrived. Later your principal will not release you. Today you see the other side: what the office's fixes actually look like when they land in your inbox. Afterwards you will say the sentence the creator uses on this call."
  • Say the warnings now, before the scene: "The EFT email to the wrong address can never be moved. The reminder is really sent. The cancel deletes the booking for good and sends an email."

You play: Yourself, Loxly at the NAPTOSA Western Cape office. You answer the phone and you also hold Finance rights. Friendly, quick and exact. While you work, you say in plain words what you're doing, and you stick to what the system really does. Don't coach her. Answer only what a real office person would.

Open with: "Good morning, NAPTOSA Western Cape office, Loxly speaking. How can I help?"

Keep these to yourself until she asks:

  • She asks if you'll resend the bank details: the first EFT instructions email went to the typo address, and it can't be sent again to the new one.
  • She asks how she'll get the payment details now: a reminder carries the same amount, reference and bank details to the corrected address.
  • She asks if the booking can be "put back" or "kept on file": a cancelled unpaid booking is deleted, not kept. Only an internal record of who removed it, when and why stays behind. Nobody can reopen it.
  • She asks what to do if she changes her mind: she books again on the same link and gets a new reference.

Stay inside these lines as the office:

  • Never send anything to a third person's address. Every email goes only to the booking's own address.
  • Never cancel a paid booking and never promise a refund.
  • Never read out or ask for real bank, card, ID, PERSAL, NAP or cell numbers. Use the placeholders.
  • Never invent a button or an email that isn't on screen.

What happens:

Part 1: The email that never came

She books before she phones. That booking is her first move in the scene.

  1. She opens the Application link and clicks "Continue".
  2. She chooses "Individual".
  3. She chooses the option that starts "Yes, member rate".
  4. In the box "NAP number (preferred) or SA ID number" she types the NAP number (her NAPTOSA membership number) and clicks "Continue". A test number the system doesn't know still books at the member rate.
    • NAP number (preferred) or SA ID number: test member NAP number 05
  5. She fills in her details, with the wrong email on purpose. She leaves PERSAL number empty, because it is optional.
    • Name: Busisiwe
    • Surname: Nkosi
    • Email address: wrong.address@example.com
    • Cell phone number: test cell number 9
  6. She clicks "Save details and continue". The toast reads "Invoice and receipt details saved. Continue to payment."
  7. On the payment step she sees "Coupon code", "Card (demonstration)" and "EFT". She clicks "EFT".
  8. She clicks "Send EFT instructions". EFT instructions is the email with NAPTOSA's bank details and her payment reference. It goes out the moment she clicks, to the address she typed.
  9. The page shows "EFT INSTRUCTIONS SENT", "Workshop registration pending EFT", the bank details, "PAYMENT STATUS Awaiting EFT" and "EMAIL STATUS Sent". The bank details are for training only. Never pay them. She writes down the line "REFERENCE (USE THIS EXACTLY)" and keeps this tab open. The EFT reference is the code a payer puts on the bank transfer so Finance can match the money. It is built from the short code, a number and the first letters of the payer's name. It should look like this:
    • Reference: EFT-DISC21-<number>-NKOSI
  10. She phones you. You answer with the opening line.
  11. She says her email was wrong and gives the right one:
    • Correct email: <Trainee personal email>
  12. You, as Finance, click "Correct payer email" and type the reason. Read the toast aloud. A toast is the short message that pops up after a click.
    • Reason: member phoned, the address had a typo
  13. She opens the inbox for <Trainee personal email> and checks the To line on each email.
  14. She says out loud that nothing new has arrived there.
  15. You, as Finance, try "Resend eft instructions email" and read the result aloud.

"Correct payer email" is a Finance-only button that changes the email address on a booking. It only redirects emails that haven't been sent yet.

Part 2: Send it to my husband (drop this part first if time is short)

  1. You say: "I've fixed the address on your booking. Is there anything else?"
  2. She asks for the bank details to go to her husband's email. If she doesn't bring him up, prompt only in character: "Who handles the payment at home?"
  3. You refuse to use any other address. You can only send to the booking's own address.
  4. You, as Finance, click "Remind" on her row. The row reads "Not reminded yet" before you click. The box asks "Send an EFT reminder to Busisiwe Nkosi?" and says "Each person can get at most one manual reminder per day." Click "Send reminder" and read the toast aloud. "Remind" sends an EFT payment reminder by hand. It works once per person per South African day.
  5. She opens the reminder in the <Trainee personal email> inbox.
  6. She reads out the amount and the reference, and finds the bank details block and the "Pay by card instead" link.
  7. She compares the reference with the one she wrote down.
  8. She does not pay and does not click "Pay by card instead". The bank details are for training only. Never pay them. Make sure of this.

Part 3: My principal won't release me

  1. You say, as Narrator: "Ten minutes later the phone rings again." Then, as the office: "Okay, the reminder's on its way. You should have it now." If you skipped part 2, say instead: "I've fixed the address on your booking. Is there anything else?"
  2. She asks to cancel because she can no longer attend.
  3. You, as Finance, click "Cancel booking" and pick the reason:
    • Reason: Can no longer attend
  4. You click "Cancel and delete booking" and read the toast aloud.
  5. She opens the cancellation email in the <Trainee personal email> inbox and reads it.
  6. She goes back to the booking tab she kept open from the start and refreshes it.

Part 4: Can you just put it back?

  1. You say: "It's cancelled now. Is there anything else I can help with?"
  2. She asks whether it can be put back. You tell her it can't.
  3. You step out of the scene as Coach and ask her for the hand-off sentence.
  4. She says, in her own words, what the creator would say on this call.
  5. Optional: show the member portal page "Workshops" for <Trainee personal email>, which reads "No workshop bookings yet".

What a good hand-off sounds like: "Only our Finance person can change the address on a booking and send the reminder. The original email can't be re-sent, so the reminder will carry the same reference and bank details to your correct address. If you cancel, Finance deletes the unpaid booking, you get one cancellation email, and no further reminders."

She should see:

  • Your toast after the correction: "Email corrected to <Trainee personal email>. No unsent emails needed to change."
  • Nothing new addressed to <Trainee personal email> after the correction.
  • The resend result: "EFT instructions email was already delivered, so it was not sent again." Emails that have already gone out are never moved.
  • Your toast after the reminder: "Reminder sent to Busisiwe Nkosi."
  • In the <Trainee personal email> inbox: a reminder whose subject starts "<N> days left before", then the workshop title, and ends "EFT payment reminder". It carries the same reference, EFT-DISC21-<number>-NKOSI.
  • Your toast after the cancel: "Booking removed for Busisiwe Nkosi. The confirmation email was sent."
  • In the <Trainee personal email> inbox: an email whose subject starts "Booking cancelled - TRAINING". It says "No payment is due and you will not receive further payment reminders for this booking."
  • The old booking tab now shows "We can't find this booking" and "It may have been cancelled. Contact NAPTOSA on 021 686 8521, or book again from the workshop page."
  • On your screen, in "All Registrations", after "Refresh", the Nkosi row is gone from "Owes money". "Owes money" is the group that lists unpaid bookings.

If she gets stuck: Wait for a real stall, point her at the hint slip first, then give these one at a time.

  • Part 1: "Before you call, write down what the booking page showed you, so you have it in front of you."
  • Part 1: "After the office says it is fixed, check for yourself where emails now land."
  • Part 2: "Ask for what Busisiwe really wants, then notice exactly what the office can and cannot do."
  • Part 2: "When an email arrives, compare its reference with the one you wrote down."
  • Part 3: "Think about what you, as the member, would want to be sure of after cancelling."
  • Part 3: "Go back to the tab you kept open from the start."
  • Part 4: "As Busisiwe, ask what you would actually do next week."
  • Part 4: "Then switch chairs: you are now the creator taking this call. What would you say?"

If it goes wrong:

  • No "Remind" button on your screen: "Remind" is hidden once a workshop has started. This workshop must be at least 3 days ahead.
  • "<person> already had a reminder sent today. Nobody was emailed again.": only one manual reminder per person per day. Read the earlier reminder instead.
  • The correction is refused with a duplicate address error: another booking on this workshop already uses that address. Correct it to her other email instead, and she checks that inbox from then on.
    • Correct email, second choice: <Trainee work email>
  • The correction toast says "Email corrected. 1 unsent email will now go to <Trainee personal email>." (or a different number): an email was still waiting to go out and will now go to the corrected address. Carry on.
  • The cancel says "Booking cancelled, but the confirmation email could not be sent. The booking was kept and marked cancelled. Tell the person yourself, then use Remove to delete it.": the row now sits in the "Cancelled" group. Tell her. You, as Finance, open "All Registrations", choose the group "Cancelled" and click "Remove" on her row. The box asks "Remove this cancelled booking?" You pick the reason and click "Remove booking". The toast reads "Booking removed for Busisiwe Nkosi." The same "Remove" is also in the Attendee ledger, the full list of bookings for one workshop.
    • Reason: Can no longer attend

Cannot be undone:

  • The EFT instructions email went to wrong.address@example.com and is never moved.
  • The reminder is really sent, and that person can't get another one today.
  • The booking is deleted for good and a cancellation email is sent.

If you are running late: This is the first block to drop. Either skip the whole scene and teach one sentence: "Correct payer email only moves emails that have not been sent yet." Or keep the scene and skip part 2, the reminder.

Debrief: After the scene, as Coach, name one specific thing that worked and one thing to try next time. Then ask:

  1. What did correcting the email change, and what did it not change? Point to what you saw in each inbox.
  2. Which of the office's actions today could you, as a creator, do yourself, and which did you hand on?
  3. What happened to the booking after the cancellation, and how would you explain that to a member who wants it back?
  4. If there's time: if a paid member phoned asking for the same cancellation, what would be different?

How did she do? Mark each one achieved, partial or missed on the scoring sheet straight away.

  • Read a corrected email correctly: done well when she checked the right inbox, said nothing new arrived after the correction, and linked that to "emails already sent are not changed". Partly when she noticed the empty inbox but expected the original email to turn up later. Missed when she didn't check the inbox, or believed the original email was re-sent.
  • Find the payment details in the reminder (don't score this if you skipped part 2): done well when she opened the reminder, read the amount, the same reference and "Pay by card instead", and didn't press it. Partly when she found the reminder but missed the reference match. Missed when she didn't find the reminder, or clicked to pay.
  • See what a cancellation leaves behind: done well when she read the line in the cancellation email and saw "We can't find this booking" on the old tab. Partly when she read the email but didn't check the old link. Missed when she expected the booking to stay visible.
  • Say the creator's hand-off sentence: done well when she said, in her own words, that Finance corrects the address and sends the reminder, the original email isn't re-sent, and a cancelled unpaid booking is deleted with one email and no more reminders. Partly when she named Finance but left out what the member will and won't receive. Missed when she couldn't say whose job it was.

11:35 · Go or stop? Four tricky requests (5 min)

Goal: She realises that some requests are hers and some she must stop and hand on. She now knows that refunds, calling off a whole workshop, fixing a wrong coupon and removing a cancelled booking are not possible for her in the app, and she knows who to pass each one to.

Worth knowing before you start: Finance can now switch a coupon off and on and change how many people may use it, and Finance can remove a cancelled booking straight from "All Registrations". None of these buttons show on her Workshop creator account, so her answer stays the same: stop and hand on.

Who drives: Her. She answers on her own. You read out each situation and its options.

Before you start:

  • This is a go-or-stop check. For each situation she decides: can I go ahead myself, or must I stop and escalate?
  • Introduce it in one line only: "Try these on your own, we will go through them after." Give no hints.
  • After each answer, read out the feedback for the option she picked, right or wrong. A wrong answer means check, fix, try again. It's never a dead end.
  • Words she will hear in the answers:
    • Finance: anyone with the Finance tick, the auditor role or a super admin. The Finance tick is the setting that gives a staff account the money buttons. In the Western Cape nobody has the Finance tick and there is no auditor, so Finance is the super admins.
    • EFT instructions: the email with NAPTOSA's bank details and a payment reference. Once it goes out, the amount on that booking is fixed.
    • Export <N> (CSV): the button in "All Registrations" that downloads a spreadsheet of the bookings. Her copy has no money columns, but it has every email address.

What happens: Ask these four, in this order.

1. The wrong coupon. A coupon is a discount code that only Finance can create or change.

  • Situation: Finance tells you they made a coupon for this workshop with the wrong price, and it was already shared with a school.
  • Question: What happens now?
  • A. Open the Coupons panel and switch the coupon off yourself. Wrong. A Workshop creator can see coupons but gets no "Edit" or "Switch off" button. Only Finance can.
  • B. Finance clicks "Edit" on the coupon and fixes the price. Wrong. "Edit" only changes how many people may use it and the note. The price and the code can't change. The box itself says "To change the price or the code, switch this coupon off and create a new one."
  • C. Stop sharing the code and hand it to Finance, the super admins in the Western Cape. They click "Switch off" on the coupon and create a new one with the right price. Right. This is a stop. Tell the school not to use the code until the office sends the new one. People who already used the old code keep their place. If an unpaid booking still holds the code, Finance sees "1 unpaid booking is using this code. Cancel that booking, or wait until it is paid, then switch the coupon off."

2. The paid refund.

  • Situation: A paid attendee emails the day before: "I can't come, please cancel and refund me."
  • Question: What can you and the system do?
  • A. Ask Finance to press "Cancel booking". The refund then goes back automatically. Wrong. "Cancel booking" only works on unpaid bookings. On a paid one the system refuses with "This booking is paid. Paid bookings need a refund and cannot be cancelled here."
  • B. The system can't do this. There is no refund control and a paid booking can't be cancelled. Pass it to Finance, who arrange any refund outside the system with the Provincial CEO's approval. Right. This is a stop. Tell the attendee Finance will contact them. Don't promise a refund or a date.
  • C. Mark them absent after the workshop. The system refunds absent people overnight. Wrong. Marking someone present or absent moves no money. At most it sends a "Thank you for attending" or "We missed you" email, and only if those emails have been switched on.
  • D. Open "Payments needing a decision" and choose Refunded. Wrong. That panel has no Refunded choice. It only shows flagged payments (like people who paid after the workshop filled up), with a note box and a "Mark resolved" button. "Mark resolved" is Finance-only, records a decision and moves no money.

3. The cancelled booking that needs removing.

  • Situation: A booking's cancellation email failed, so the booking now sits in the "Cancelled" group in "All Registrations". It needs to be removed.
  • Question: Who does it, and where?
  • A. Finance, from "All Registrations" > the group "Cancelled" > "Remove" on that row > pick a reason > "Remove booking". Right. "Remove" is a Finance action, and it only shows when the cancelled booking has no payment history. The same button is also in the Attendee ledger (the full list of bookings for one workshop). If the booking has a payment history, the row reads "Kept for the money record: this booking has a payment history." and nobody can remove it.
  • B. You, from the cancelled row in "All Registrations" > "Remove". Wrong. "Remove" is Finance-only. On her Workshop creator account it doesn't show.
  • C. Nobody. Cancelled bookings delete themselves overnight. Wrong. It stays until Finance removes it. Also tell the person yourself, because their cancellation email didn't go.
  • D. You press "Close registrations", which clears out cancelled bookings. Wrong. Closing keeps every record with the workshop. It removes nothing.

4. The whole workshop is called off.

  • Situation: The venue fell through and the whole workshop must be called off. 12 people have already paid and 3 have not.
  • Question: What do you do?
  • A. Press "Cancel workshop". It emails and refunds every payer. Wrong. There is no such button. The buttons are "Publish workshop", "Close registrations", the two Reopen buttons and "Delete workshop".
  • B. Press "Delete workshop" so nobody can book. Wrong. 12 people have paid, so it refuses: "A payment has been recorded for this workshop, so it cannot be deleted. Payment records are always kept." There is no "Cancel workshop" button, so the way to stop new bookings is "Close registrations". Even with nobody paid, deleting wipes every booking and emails nobody.
  • C. Press "Close registrations". Export the payers with "Export <N> (CSV)" and contact each person yourself. Hand the 3 unpaid bookings to Finance to cancel. Escalate the 12 refunds to Finance and the Provincial CEO. Right. Closing stops new bookings and keeps every record in "Closed archive". The notices and the refunds are done by hand.
  • D. Use "Edit details" to move the date to next year so the reminders stop. Wrong. That hides the problem. Nobody is told, and the paid people still expect to attend.

If you have time left, use these from the same set:

  • The price went up after she booked. You raise the non-member price from R 300 to R 350, three weeks before the workshop. Mpho booked at R 300 last week, got EFT instructions and hasn't paid. What does she owe now? Right answer: R 300. Her amount was fixed when her EFT instructions went out. Traps: R 350 (no, the band in "Edit details" says a price change affects future registrations only). "Nothing, her booking is cancelled" (no, editing a workshop never cancels a booking, only Finance's "Cancel booking" does). "Finance must re-price it in Money history" (no, Money history is a read-only record).
  • The venue moved. A workshop has 9 bookings, 4 paid. You moved the venue and saw "Workshop updated." What do you do so people know? Right answer: use "Export <N> (CSV)" in "All Registrations" to get every payer's email, then write to them yourself from the office mailbox. Traps: "Nothing, the system emails everyone" (saving an edit sends no email). "Ask Finance to press Remind for everyone" (that's a payment reminder for unpaid bookings only, paid people get nothing, and it isn't a change notice).
  • A school wants a different contact email. EFT instructions were already sent and you don't have the Finance tick. Right answer: hand it to Finance. They use "All Registrations" > the row's "…" > "View details" > "Correct payer email", with a reason. "Correct payer email" is the Finance-only button that changes the email address on a booking. Tell the school that emails already sent won't be re-sent, but future emails go to the new address. Traps: changing it in "Edit details" (that changes the workshop, not a booking). Asking the school to book again (that makes a second booking that also gets chased, and the first one still owes). Changing the reminder's recipient (reminders always go to the booking's own address).
  • A paid non-member wants to move to next month's workshop. Right answer: tell her the system can't move a paid booking. Take her details and hand it to Finance, who decide with the Provincial CEO on a refund or another arrangement. Tell her Finance will contact her. Traps: changing the workshop on her booking (no screen does that). Sending next month's link and saying this month's booking cancels itself (nothing cancels itself, and she would pay twice). Asking Finance to press "Cancel booking" (her booking is paid, so it's refused).
  • The workshop is full and an unpaid teacher still wants to come. She has an email titled "Capacity reached - <title> - please do not pay", and her booking page says "Please do not send payment". Right answer: check the workshop's "Capacity" first. If management agrees to more seats, raise "Capacity" in "Edit details" > "2. Delivery & Pricing" and click "Save changes", then tell her yourself she may pay with the reference she already has. If not, tell her not to pay and hand the booking to Finance to cancel. Why: the "please do not pay" email goes to every unpaid booking the moment paid seats fill the workshop. More seats is a management call (room, facilitator, materials). If it stays full, Finance's row reads "This workshop is full. Refund this person, then use Cancel booking on this row." She hasn't paid, so Finance only cancels, and she gets one "Booking cancelled" email. Traps: "Pay anyway" (while it's full the system won't let Finance mark her paid, and her money would need a refund outside the system). Raising capacity without asking (you can, but it isn't your decision). "The system will email you when a seat opens" (there is no such email, and the "please do not pay" email is not taken back).

What the system cannot do, and what to do instead (read these out if she asks):

  • Delete a workshop that someone has paid for. "Delete workshop" only works while nobody has paid. It asks for a reason and a tick on "I understand this cannot be undone.", then deletes the workshop, its link, its files and every unpaid booking for good, and emails nobody. Once any payment exists it refuses. Press "Close registrations" instead. A closed workshop sits in "Closed archive" with all its records. A draft that was never published can be deleted, or just left in "Drafts".
  • Keep a finished workshop open. Since 08 Oct the system closes every published workshop at 00:05 the night after it ends. A closed workshop refuses new bookings, an unpaid booking on it can no longer get EFT details or pay by card, and its payment reminders stop. Pressing "Reopen registrations" on a workshop whose dates have passed is undone at the next 00:05 run. If the event moved, use "Edit details" to set the new dates. Until that night's run the link still takes bookings, so check "Needs closure" after each workshop and close what is done.
  • Call off a whole workshop and tell everyone, or email everyone about a change. Neither exists, and saving "Edit details" sends nothing. Press "Close registrations". Use "Export <N> (CSV)" to get every payer's email and contact them yourself from the office mailbox. Hand the unpaid bookings to Finance to cancel. Each person then gets a "Booking cancelled" email that says it was cancelled as requested, so write to them first. Hand paid people to Finance and the Provincial CEO for refunds.
  • Refund someone, or cancel a paid booking. There's no refund control, and "Cancel booking" refuses a paid booking. Tell the person you're passing it to Finance. Finance arranges the refund by bank transfer outside the system, with the Provincial CEO's approval. Don't promise a date or an amount.
  • Take a part payment, a deposit, or cash or card at the door. Every payment is recorded for the full amount. The person pays the full amount by EFT. A walk-in books on the public page like anyone else, and Finance marks the EFT paid when it arrives.
  • Remove a cancelled booking that has a payment history. Its row reads "Kept for the money record: this booking has a payment history." Money records are never destroyed. A cancelled booking with no payment history can go: hand it to Finance, who click "Remove" on the row in "All Registrations" > "Cancelled", pick a reason and click "Remove booking". She doesn't get this button.
  • Change a coupon's price or code. Finance can only change how many people may use a coupon and its note, or switch it off and on. For a wrong price, Finance switches the coupon off and creates a new one. Tell Finance straight away, the super admins in the Western Cape. Don't share the code in the meantime.
  • Change the number of people, the name or the coupon on a booking after EFT instructions went out, or move a booking to another workshop. The booking is locked ("EFT instructions have already been issued. Contact staff to change this booking.") and no staff screen edits a headcount or moves a booking. Unpaid: Finance cancels it and the person books again on the right Application link. Paid: it needs a refund decision, so hand it to Finance and the Provincial CEO.
  • Send the original EFT instructions or receipt to a new address, or to someone else (a husband, a bursar). "Correct payer email" only moves emails that haven't been sent yet, and every email goes only to the booking's own address. Finance corrects the address and sends a "Remind" (for an unpaid EFT), which carries the same reference and bank details to the new address. The person forwards it themselves if someone else is paying.

If she asks about the "Payments needing a decision" panel:

  • It sits on "Published Links", just above "Registrations, coupons and documents", for the selected workshop. It only appears when something needs a decision. On most workshops she won't see it.
  • Each line shows the person's name, the amount and the reason, for example "Paid after available seats filled. Open another seat or arrange a refund."
  • As a creator she sees "Only a super admin or the finance (auditor) role can close this." There's no note box and no button for her. In the Western Cape there is no auditor, so that means the super admins.
  • In "All Registrations", the same person shows a row badge "Paid R <amount> · seat needs review".
  • If Finance shows her their screen, a pending EFT row on a full workshop reads "This workshop is full. Refund this person, then use Cancel booking on this row." That's Finance's instruction too, not hers.
  • Her job: send Finance a short message with the workshop title, the person's name, the amount and what she saw. Then tell the person only what's true: NAPTOSA has their payment and will contact them to confirm a seat or arrange a refund.
  • When Finance types a note and clicks "Mark resolved", the toast reads "Marked as resolved." and the line disappears. The panel goes once nothing is left. "Mark resolved" sends no email, so Finance or the office must contact the person.
  • If she promised a refund on the spot: she calls back and corrects it. Refunds happen outside the system and need Finance and the Provincial CEO.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block.

If you are running late: Ask only the paid refund, the cancelled booking and the whole workshop questions.

11:40 · Break (10 min)

Goal: She rests. Nothing to learn here. You use the time to set up the next part.

Who drives: You, on your second screen. Nothing here is permanent.

  • On "TRAINING - Classroom Management Essentials - delete after" (today's workshop): Palesa Khoza is still in "Check in" (the group of paid people waiting to arrive) and Aisha Abrahams is still in "Owes money" (the group of unpaid bookings). That's how it looked at 09:00.
  • On "TRAINING - Reading Recovery - delete after" (tomorrow's workshop): check-in is not open. There's no "Opened early by" line.
  • Get the phone ready with the door QR code for today's workshop. That's the code that goes on the hall door and opens the check-in page.

If you are running late: Cut the break to 7 minutes.

Part 6: On the day, attendance, certificates and exports (11:50 to 12:25)

This part is the workshop day itself. Members judge NAPTOSA on this day, and their CPTD points depend on two things: attendance and the certificate. Attendance needs a staff click, or the person checking in with the QR at the door. The certificate goes out by itself at 08:00 the morning after the workshop, to everyone who paid and was marked present. Staff can send it sooner. Two kinds of email stay off unless a super admin switched them on: the reminder before the workshop, and the email when someone is marked present or absent. She cannot switch them on herself. Registration closes by itself at 00:05 the night after the workshop ends, not the moment it ends. Money is still not her job on the day. An unpaid person at the door goes to Finance, and her export has no amounts in it.

Two training workshops are used in this part:

  • Today's workshop: "TRAINING - Classroom Management Essentials - delete after"
    • Date and time: Thu 08 Oct 2026, 08:00 to 16:30
    • Venue: Silvertree Primary School hall (in person)
    • Presenter (the Facilitator field): Mrs Charmaine Adonis
    • CPTD points: 5
  • Tomorrow's workshop: "TRAINING - Reading Recovery - delete after"
    • Date and time: Fri 09 Oct 2026, 09:00 to 12:00
    • Bookings: none

You made every booking on today's workshop the night before. All of them use loxly@lbtec.co.za except Xolani Ngcobo's school place, which uses <Trainee work email>, because a teacher on a school place cannot use an email that already has a paid booking on the workshop. Every other email lands in your inbox. None of the bookings she made earlier in the day is on this workshop.

Goal: She learns that on the workshop day one screen shows everyone who booked, sorted by what still has to happen to them. She now knows that the paid people she is waiting for sit in "Check in".

Who drives: Her screen. You talk.

'Certificate to send' is people marked present. 'Done' is people who have their certificate or were marked absent. Cancelled bookings sit in 'Cancelled'."

11:52 · Get the door ready and send the morning email (7 min)

Goal: She learns that the door QR is how paid people check themselves in, and that she can open check-in early to test it and then close it again. She now knows that the "Workshop starts today" email can only go on the workshop's own day and only reaches paid people. She also knows that a reminder before the workshop only goes out if a super admin has switched on "Workshop starting soon".

Who drives: Both. She works on her screen. You scan the QR with your phone.

Before you start:

  • Your phone, ready to scan a QR.
  • Your inbox open:
    • Inbox: loxly@lbtec.co.za
  • Her account has the Workshop creator tick. That is the permission that lets a staff member run workshops. She needs it to open check-in early and to send the email. Anyone on staff in the province can open the dialog, copy the link and download the QR.

What happens:

Tomorrow's workshop: test the door QR

When a certificate is issued, the name as booked, the workshop title, the dates, the facilitator, the CPTD points and the SACE number are copied onto it with a certificate number. Editing the workshop later never changes it, and it cannot be withdrawn. The dialog says 'This cannot be undone.'

Certificates also go out by themselves. At 08:00 the morning after a workshop's last day, everyone who paid and was marked present, and has no certificate yet, gets one by email. It goes once only. In "Published Links", the workshop's "Certificates" panel reads 'Automatic certificates, all provinces: On since 08 Oct 2026'. She should see:

  • After Thandi's certificate: the toast 'Certificate CERT-WS-<date>-<SC>-<INITIAL+SURNAME> issued. Emailed to the attendee.' and the row in "Done". The certificate number is built from the date, the workshop's short code (SC) and the person's initial and surname. The short code is a code of up to 6 letters the system builds from the workshop title.
  • After "Send 1": the toast '1 certificate issued.' It says 'certificates' when more than one goes.

If she gets stuck: give one hint at a time.

If it goes wrong:

  • A warning that stays on screen, saying the certificate was issued but the email did not send: She goes to "Done", opens the row's "..." menu and chooses "Resend certificate email". A failed row also shows a red '... Certificate email failed' and an outline "Resend certificate email" button.
  • 'Certificate email could not be sent. <reason>' on resend: She reads the reason. A bad address needs "Correct payer email", which is a Finance action.
  • An error starting 'certificate: ... has not paid for this workshop': The person is unpaid. Finance first, then attendance, then the certificate.
  • An error starting 'certificate: ... is not marked as attended': She marks them "Present" first.
  • The 'This covers ...' line names more than one workshop: She clicks "Cancel", picks this workshop in the "Workshop" select and tries again.
  • She cannot find a person in "Certificate to send": They are unpaid ("Owes money"), not marked present ("Check in"), absent or already sent ("Done"), or it is a school row. Schools never get certificates. Teachers get them in their own names.
  • Watch out for the ledger button. The Attendee ledger is the full list of the workshop's bookings, under "Published Links". Its "Issue & email certificate" button sends straight away, with no 'This cannot be undone' dialog. Check the name before clicking it.

If she is asked for something the system cannot do:

  • "Send the automatic certificates tonight, or change their 08:00 time.": The time is fixed at 08:00 the morning after the last day, for all provinces, and nobody can change it. To send tonight, she uses "Send certificate" on each row or "Send all N" for the workshop. "Run now" on the "Certificates" panel is for a super admin only, and only once the workshop has ended.
  • "Withdraw a certificate, or change the name, points, SACE number or dates on one already issued.": Issuing is final ('This cannot be undone.'). Those facts are frozen when it is issued, and editing the workshop later never changes them. No screen lets any staff member edit a booked name, super admin included. Check the name before issuing. If it is wrong, do not issue. Log it with the system administrator as a data fix, and issue once it is fixed. The automatic run sends it at 08:00 the morning after with the name as booked, so the fix has to happen before then.
  • "Keep the booking link open for late bookings the day after the workshop.": It cannot. At 00:05 tonight the nightly run closes every workshop that ended before midnight, this one included. After that, new bookings are refused, an unpaid booking can no longer get EFT details or pay by card, and its payment reminders stop. Pressing "Reopen registrations" on it is undone at the next 00:05 run. Until tonight's run, after 16:30 the website's Events page drops the workshop on its own, but the booking link and its QR code still take bookings. In "Published Links" the workshop moves to "Needs closure", which reads 'N past dates still open'. To stop bookings sooner she selects it there and clicks "Close registrations". She can, because she has the Workshop creator tick, and so can a super admin. The workshop then moves to "Closed archive" and keeps every booking, payment, attendance mark and certificate.
  • "Link my portal account to a booking made with another email, so my certificate shows in the portal.": The portal only matches the confirmed sign-in email, and the staff tool to link them is not built. She finds the person in All Registrations, uses "Resend certificate email" to the booking address, and helps by phone or email.
  • "Show me how an online or hybrid attendee gets the joining link, and the check-in link in their morning email.": Not practised today. This workshop is in person, so its email has no link. It is the first self-paced follow-up lesson. Until she has done it, she asks the facilitator before setting up an online or hybrid workshop.

Cannot be undone: Palesa's certificate (issued by you), Thandi's and Hennie's are issued for good. The name as booked, points, SACE number and dates are frozen, the certificates cannot be withdrawn, and the emails cannot be called back. "Send all" runs once on this workshop, never again. The resend sends nothing new.

If you are running late: Never drop this scene. Skip the SACE points question, because the 12:21 block covers it.

Debrief:

  1. "When Thandi asked for her name to change, what did you say before you clicked? What is frozen on a certificate when it is issued?"

  • Extra check: "You just sent Thandi's certificate. What was frozen on it, and what happens if someone edits the workshop's CPTD points tomorrow?" Right answer: her name exactly as booked, the workshop title, the dates, the facilitator, the CPTD points, the SACE number and the certificate number. Editing the workshop tomorrow changes nothing on her certificate, and it cannot be withdrawn.

How did she do?

12:23 · Self-check quiz (2 min)

Goal: She now knows that any staff member except read-only and bot accounts may send a certificate. She learns that an unpaid person with cash at the door goes to Finance, not onto the register.

Who drives: Her. You read each situation and the choices, and she answers.

Before you start:

  • Two words she needs. The Finance tick is the permission that lets someone mark an EFT payment as paid. EFT is a bank transfer, and each booking has its own reference so Finance can match the money on the bank statement. In the Western Cape, Finance means the super admins.

Question 1

  • Situation: Two people sit in "Certificate to send". A colleague who is a case manager (not read-only, no Finance tick) asks if she may send them while you are on a call.
  • Question: What is true?
  • Choices:
    • A. Only Finance may send certificates, and they can withdraw one later.
    • B. Only the super admin may send them, because CPTD points are printed on them.
    • C. Any staff except read-only and bot may click "Send certificate". Once issued it cannot be undone, and later workshop edits never change it.
    • D. Only the creator who built the workshop may send them, and a second click deletes the first one.
  • Right answer: C. She may send them. Ask her to check the names first, because the name as booked is frozen on the certificate. If nobody sends them, they go out by themselves at 08:00 the morning after the workshop.
  • Why the others are wrong:
    • A: Certificates need no Finance tick, and none can be withdrawn. The dialog says 'This cannot be undone.'
    • B: Points on the certificate do not change who may click. Any staff except read-only and bot may.
    • D: Any staff except read-only and bot may send, and nothing deletes an issued certificate. "Resend certificate email" only sends the email again.

Question 2

  • Situation: A teacher at the door says she paid by EFT last night. Her row is in "Owes money" with 'Attendance opens once the EFT is marked paid'. She offers R250 cash.
  • Question: What do you do?
  • Choices:
    • A. Take the cash and click "Present". Finance can sort out the money later.
    • B. Hand it to Finance: ask her to email proof of payment, and once Finance has marked the EFT paid, mark her "Present". She can go in and sit down meanwhile.
    • C. Ask Finance to mark her paid now because she promised.
    • D. Send her away. Unpaid people may not attend.
  • Right answer: B. "Mark EFT paid" is Finance only, and Finance marks it once the reference shows on the bank statement. Then attendance opens and she marks her present.
  • Why the others are wrong:
    • A: Her row has no "Present" button, and there is no cash or door payment in the system.
    • C: "Mark EFT paid" sends a receipt straight away and cannot be undone. Finance only marks it when the money is on the statement.
    • D: Whether she may sit in is not something the app decides. The app only blocks marking her present until Finance confirms payment.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block.

If you are running late: Move both questions into the workbook.

Part 7: Capstone, one workshop end to end, and the close (12:25 to 13:10)

This is the last part of the day. She runs one brand-new workshop from start to finish on her own, from her own planning sheet. You play the Provincial CEO, the member who books, and Finance. Then you debrief, and she writes down what she learned.
